The painter believes this must be the work of "some magnificent god of art" who manifested to guide him.

### 3210406 · 多么可恨的命运

> What a Cruel Fate

???：What a cruel "Storm."

???：So far, the story has followed along a classic direction—Brume walked "the Hero's Journey."

???：But just as it seemed that it might end with triumph, down came the "Storm."

???：This is the fifth joke fate has played on me.

???：I must be honest with you. There is something in it that I like.

???：As dreadful as it may be to stand before a future I cannot foresee, I feel something else. Perhaps you might call it hope.

???：For someone born with my abilities, there is a strange beauty in moments like these, to know how it feels to know nothing.

???：Of course you did. You know the "Storm" better than most anyone.

???：I suspect you knew how it would end the moment I told you how it began.

???：Yet I cannot see beyond the "Storm," so it always takes me by surprise.

???：Yes, though I understand it may come at a great price.

???：Ah! Perhaps that is why the Vigiles Bureau and the Foundation have kept such a keen eye on me.

???：Perhaps they think me one of those "Storm" advocates.

???：But why would I advocate for it? I have never asked for it. All I have done is try to understand it.

### 3210301 · On the Book Cover

In an old bookstore on Rue Saint-Honoré, a faded book draws your attention. Its contents are exactly what you want to understand. You brush your fingers once more across the gold-stamped title.

Salon Etiquette—Written by a Court Attendant

Yes. A book that helps you blend into society.

The Beasts of North Africa

Yes. A book that awakens the wild.

The Lover Holding Lilies

Yes. A book that lights two souls at once.

Elements of Geometry

Yes. A book that questions the nature of the world.

### 3210407 · 虽然并非皆大欢喜

> Not Every Story Ends in Joy

???：It was then that I decided to help Brume one last time.

???：There was risk in my choice. I could not see what the consequences would be.

???：Still, I succeeded, and the rewards were most generous. Many suspected that I had planned it from the start—a common accusation made of diviners, of course.

???：Do you think I did not punish her enough?

???：She lives, true, but sealed inside a false diamond. I doubt that was the life she had intended.

???：Besides, Brume has the right to decide her fate. It was not my place to choose whether she lives or dies.

???：Thank you for your concern; I am doing well, though the future now comes to me through an ever-thickening haze.

???：Though, I should say, I find myself welcoming the lack of clarity.

???：I am now able to watch my daughter grow, to observe her, and feel true surprise in every step she takes.

???：If there is anything to take away from Brume's story, I would suggest this:

???：Sometimes, good can come from being blind to fate's path.

???：How exciting it must be to arrive in this world knowing nothing and, after a long, beautiful journey, realize just how much there is still to discover.

### 3210106 · Open the Door of Fate

**A Prophecy Comes True**

She must have foreseen this moment before.  
The clean lines of the dark marble floor before her match the image in her mind. The visitors' shapes are just as they appeared in the orbuculum. The woman with black-rimmed glasses and dark eyes is kinder but firmer than expected. Blue top hat, distant look—a familiar childhood friend.  
And then ... a duckling. She flaps her wings as she rushes up the steps, the sound of leather shoes stomping loudly. She doesn't apologize for bumping into anyone ... First floor, second. The hallways of the Foundation seem endless today ... No need to ask for directions, she runs, panting, sure of where she's going ...

"Please leave the door, Vertin. I'm expecting more company," she says quietly.  
A glance passes between the one in the blue hat and the one with glasses, who gives a slight nod.

Footsteps approach quickly, accompanied by the sound of labored breathing. There's no knock. The door, already slightly ajar, is pushed wide open.  
"Ha! Mama! *deep breath* I knew I'd find you here!"

### 3210107 · The Opposite of Perjury

???：Ah, a visitor. How unexpected. There is nothing here now—only the ashes of war. What is it you want from a place like this?

???：Run along now. Back to your schoolbooks ... or a swing in a playground, perhaps. This is no place for a girl like you.

???：Naturally. I am no celebrity.

???：I'd prefer to keep it that way.

???：Fame is of no interest to me. Just an opportunity for greater harassment.

???：Then you and I are in agreement.

???：The stench of rust and burnt flesh is quite unbearable.

???：Even the maggots squirming over the soldiers' corpses have summoned all their strength just to crawl away.

???：But the entire world is in ruins now. I have nowhere else to go.

???：And it seems you're stuck here with me. You poor sap.

???：You trekked to the end of the world, fought a pointless war, and for what? A broken spinning wheel?

???：Even that, they took away from you—along with the girl clinging to life by a thread—before sending you off to the next battleground.

???：I hope that rusty old thing holds SOME kind of secret—to saving the world, or ending this war.

???：Maybe then, you can take on another career before you die. A watchmaker, perhaps.

???：I've nothing more to say. Would you kindly leave me alone for a while?

???：I have no tea or snacks to offer. I can't even recall what they taste like.

???：Go on. Savor the war. There'll be another after this. No need to worry about things going exactly as you'd like.

### 3510101 · Mutual Assistance

???：Ah, we meet again—though it isn't much of a surprise.

???：Your move from the Western Front to the eastern one is utterly predictable.

???：Running around like a lost chick, following after her mother hen—or really anything big enough to grab your attention.

???：Will you ever learn to stand on your own? When will you grow up?

???：And then what? Watch her disappear again?

???：You're not an idiot, so why do you keep making the same mistakes?

???：You aren't the type to find joy in running on a treadmill, are you?

???：Then again—maybe you are that type after all, at least when it comes to chasing fashion.

???：What's that supposed to mean? Of course I understand family. I know what "love" is!

???：She attended to your every need when you were still wet behind the wings. You depended on her, clung to her—because your very survival demanded it.

???：Then one day, she was gone from your life. You had to fly on your own, do your own chores. You wept over the "good old days" of comfort and being cared for—oh, poor birdie.

???：But the truth is, that care and affection were nothing more than her way of feeding a sick obsession with control. That's what "love" is—shallow as a birdbath!

???：I was "loved" once. I "loved" others, too. I've experienced it firsthand, and I understand it as well as anyone!

???：Ah...

???：She's key to saving the world—that's what you're saying? I'm tired of it.

???：Tired of sick jokes like this, with self-important ideologues bragging about securing a bright future for everyone.

???：You hear any cheers or laughter? Nope—because everyone who believed in their causes already died fighting for them.

???：If you ask me, we should hang some of those "big shots" in public. It wouldn't bring peace, of course, but maybe the rest of us would feel a little relief ...

???：Come, take a seat. I've set your favorite chair facing east.

???：The view's better that way, not to mention that the river helps wash away the stench of corpses.

### 3510102 · Absolute Luck

**Witch for a Day's First Mexico Tour**

The beautiful, passionate young woman.  
The Universal Star kissed by a lucky coin.  
Witch for a Day's most beloved old friend—Ms. Centurion—will be in Mexico City on the fifteenth of this month.

You'll find her at Plaza Garibaldi during the day, chatting with street musicians and sharing drinks with hungover drunks. She remembers everyone's name, every unfulfilled wish, and every unfinished adventure. She brings the very best stories to the show. When night falls, she'll appear at the gambling tables. If you spot her tossing a coin into a drain near the Zócalo, don't worry—it's part of the routine. It always comes back.

During the tour, she'll make a brief stop in Xochimilco to mix "the most challenging cocktail of the century" with hot sauce and apple juice. From there, she'll head to the lakeside to make her death-defying leap. As the locals scream, the legend of the girl with the golden coin will reach unprecedented heights. Of course, we can't rule out the possibility that she'll hop aboard a passing train and turn it into an impromptu adventure show.

Note for the Audience:  
After the show, if you've lost all sense of what "luck" even means, take that as proof you've fully enjoyed her performance.

### 3510103 · Festival Visitors

**From El Semanario Heraldo de México**

Yesterday afternoon, a foreign diplomat arrived in Mexico City. His motorcade proceeded along Bucareli Street into the city center, making a brief stop at the Zócalo. Streetcar bells echoed through the streets as vendors laid out bundles of marigolds ahead of Día de los Fieles Difuntos. The gentleman is said to be no stranger to Aztec mythology, the Sun Stone, and the origins of the festival.

Later in the day, he visited the Academia de San Carlos, where he commented briefly on the public murals that have recently drawn attention. His interest, however, extended well beyond the academy walls. At a quiet corner of La Merced Market, the guest paused before several shop windows, taking in the prints, candy skulls, and papel picado on display. Though simply crafted, each piece carried a rich emotional weight. One piece held his attention above all others—a calavera figurine engraved with satirical verse. He turned it over repeatedly, praising it without reserve, and offered no reply even as his entourage urged him onward.

No further details regarding the diplomat's remaining itinerary have been released. Nevertheless, during this festival when the dead return and the living rejoice, our distinguished visitor has proven himself far more than a passing observer. Mexico City may soon count among its guests an uncommon connoisseur of the arts.

Reported by Fitzwilliam

### 3510106 · "This city is ours."

**The City and the "City"**

After the "Storm," the Vigiles Bureau returns to every "important city" following the same pattern—an unremarkable office at first, then a few Vigils, and before long, people simply grow accustomed to its presence as though it has always belonged there.

According to official records, the Vigiles Bureau exists to investigate complex cases involving arcanum. It operates independently of the government, each fulfilling its own mandate without interfering with the other's affairs, though the two maintain steady communication.

At the same time, the criminal underworld enters what the press calls the age of "modern crime." The most dangerous example of which is known as "El Automóvil Gris" or the Grey Car Gang, driving in their eponymous automobiles with matching grey attire. Their increasingly organized robberies and violence have given them a steady spot in the crime columns. Their methods have been dissected and debated at length, yet no amount of analysis softens the reality of what they are. People have begun to realize that violence and plunder are becoming more organized and more efficient. Crime is no longer a matter for the police alone—it has become a reality the whole of society can no longer afford to ignore.

Of course, the Grey Car Gang alone is not enough to shake the order of an entire city. The local police know the limits of their authority, and the Vigiles Bureau understands how to avoid raising questions outside its jurisdiction. The underworld and the authorities share the same map—they simply disagree on how to read it.

This seemingly harmonious coexistence does not eliminate conflict, only confines it. Police, Vigils, and grey cars pass through the same streets, fully aware of one another. They all understand the same unspoken truth—when the sun rises, the streets return to normal. Everything dissolves into the daylight, folding back into the city's shadow.

### 3510107 · Beneath the Dust

**The Muralism Movement, Part I**

In early twentieth-century Mexico, no art form is more woven into everyday life than the art of the mural. Too large for any private collection, they occupy stairwells, courtyards, and corridors. It is for this reason that the muralism movement sparks continuous debate—about art itself, about who gets to decide how art is presented, and about how long these works can remain in public spaces.

There was a time when the government dictated the direction of aesthetics. Only when the old order collapses do suppressed traditions find new life. Public buildings become canvases. Images naturally take on the role of narrative and expression. Murals spread across corridors and courtyards, functioning less like paintings meant to be admired and more like street signs or door plaques. Across different works, the same gestures appear again and again—heads raised, hands at labor, bodies in motion. Learning to read these images becomes part of life in the city and beyond. Through this art, Mexicans begin to build an entirely new sense of identity.

In technique, most of these works take the form of frescos. The painter works against the drying plaster. Pigments sink into the wall and allow almost no revision once set. Over time, colors gradually fade and outlines disappear beneath dust. Countless people pass beneath them, look up briefly, and walk on. Yet the walls remain, still bearing the traces.

Perhaps the mural's true significance lies not in its form or technique, but in the emotion and memory it carries.

### 3510112 · Cleansing Notes

**The "Necessary" Guide to Exorcism**

1. Agave

Handling Note: Mind your hands around the large leaves. They're sharp.  
Practical Uses: Commonly used in brewing, medicine, and rituals. Its fermented sap produces pulque, a drink Mexicans call "the lifeblood of the land."  
Exorcism Uses: Smear the sap on door frames and floors after a space has been "cleansed" to shut out any "unseen presences."

2. Epazote

No special handling notes.  
Practical Uses: Aids digestion and can expel or kill intestinal parasites.  
Exorcism Uses: After an exorcism, if "their presence" still lingers in the room, sprinkle some epazote. Let's just say it smells as "special" as it sounds.

3. Rue

No special handling notes.  
Practical Uses: Commonly used to ward off evil. Can be worn on the body, brewed as tea, or sprinkled on the ground.  
Exorcism Uses: The final step of an "exorcism," it helps with sealing against future incursions.

4. Copal

Caution: Make sure the smoke spreads throughout the entire room.  
Practical Uses: A natural resin native to Central America, used in sacred rituals since the time of the Aztecs and the Maya. Now widely used during Día de los Fieles Difuntos as a medium to connect the living with the dead.  
Exorcism Uses: As copal smoke rises, "they" find their way back to where they belong.

5. Rosemary

For the living only.  
Practical Uses: Healing.  
Exorcism Uses: If the "scene" feels wrong, or you're dealing with a survivor who's lost control, a little rosemary helps you find yourself again faster.

### 3510113 · Ancient Calendar

**The Story of the Sun Stone**

The Aztec Sun Stone is often called the "Aztec Calendar." But it's really more of a cosmic map carved in basalt—or, as the Aztecs understood it, a guide to how the world works.

The stone was unearthed in Mexico City in the 18th century. It measures over 3.5 meters across and weighs more than 20 tons. The center of the Sun Stone is Tōnatiuh, the god of the sun. His gaze is stern, his outstretched tongue carved to resemble a Tecpatl. The message is clear: offer the sun your most precious gift—your blood—or receive nothing in return.

The concentric rings around Tōnatiuh record the four "suns" that came before—one transformed into a jaguar and devoured the people, one engulfed the world in a hurricane, one rained fire from the heavens, and the last drowned the world in a massive flood. Each sun's reign ended in an apocalypse, and none of their subjects survived.

Moving outward, you'll find religious symbols, patterns marking the cycle of day and night, and colors representing celestial bodies ... They unfold layer by layer, like a cosmic machine disassembled and laid bare—precise, ancient, orderly. Together, they capture the Aztec understanding of the world: the universe resets, all things are reborn, and humanity is nothing more than a speck of dust within it.

### 3510118 · The Necessary Step

**Murals and Turning Points**

This is a land where traditions are told through murals. After the revolution, the young government turned to its artists. This newborn Mexico had so many stories to tell, so many to shape—and paintings adorning the walls of its buildings became the perfect medium. In the 1920s, the muralist movement swept through the country.

Look, this was an art form both ancient and brand new. It no longer lived in auction houses, galleries, or the private collections of the wealthy. Instead, it unfolded across vast walls open to the public. On those walls, artists spoke of justice, of freedom, of this nation's history and memory. Imagine it! A new country, a new canvas, and so many stories to tell! From the very beginning, these murals were inseparable from politics. The brush was the artist's voice. They spoke for their ideals, for beauty, for all the suffering people of the world ...

But voices are difficult to harmonize. And they can turn harsh.

As the 20th century entered its third decade, cracks began to appear—between artist and artist, between artist and state. Should the murals depict suffering or glory? Whose praises should they sing? Which path forward? Rivera, Orozco, and Siqueiros—three names that once symbolized hope, our "Los Tres Grandes"—held different visions for the future of both their art and their country.

The rifts only deepened. In the small "Midnight Kitchen," young painters listened to this "fight of the century."

## 章节 36101

### 3610101 · School Days

**String Theory 101**

A philosopher from your homeland once said, "If we take a foot-long stick and cut it in half each day, we will never reach its end."  
No, I'm not here to discuss calculus. I'd like to ask you this: Do you think a stick can truly be divided forever? If you reach the point where it can no longer be halved, what remains?  
You might say it's some fundamental particle, but in my class, the correct answer is a string.  
Nature's most basic unit is not a particle but an incredibly tiny string. Divide a stick to its limit, and you'll find a string. Divide gold down to its limit, and you'll also find a string. Even the basic unit of gravity should be a string.  
The entire world is made of strings vibrating at different frequencies in unseen, higher dimensions. They may seem insignificant, but they are everywhere.  
Some pragmatists may laugh at you for taking this class. Why deal with a fantastical theory that has no experimental proof?  
I am a human, but you are an arcanist. I hope you understand my pursuit of a perfect concept, Ms. Zhang.

Step 4: Make sure you have at least one friend to play with—multiple personalities count as friends too!

### 3610104 · An Unfinished Book

**Sequel**

After that, the "Storm" brought the world to the 1990s.  
Ms. Isolde readily accepted treatment. Ms. Kakania has been traveling the world, striving to change it. She quickly adapted to the new era and read many books. I also gave her plenty of recommendations.  
It is worth noting that after the Vienna incident, Ms. Semmelweis became a vampire. This came as a massive surprise to everyone, but it also fascinated me—like reaching the payoff for a story's foreshadowing. After making many sincere requests, I got permission to read the beginning of her story. It is a complex tale, one that leaves you pondering incessantly about how it ends. However, if I were to retell the whole story here, this book would end up three times thicker. I would like to thank my mentor, Madam Hofmann, for showing me how to keep things concise in situations like this.  
...  
The world has traveled to 1920, but the war has not ended. It all feels like a continuation of that chaotic day. I heard Ms. Kakania was dejected the whole day, but she quickly returned to her mission from the Field Agent Administration. Even after crossing two eras, her tenacious spirit has not dampened at all. It seems that spirit has affected me, too: I find myself constantly thinking about what I can do. Thus, I have decided to write this book to organize my thoughts, in the hopes that I will find a conclusion before the final draft.  
...

### 3610105 · Researcher Onboarding Guide

**Beginner's Guide to Ciphers: Vigenère Edition**

To prevent Laplace employees from falling victim to pranks from colleagues and to avoid research delays from being unable to break codes, we are distributing this Beginner's Guide to Ciphers: Vigenère Edition.  
The Vigenère cipher can be seen as an upgrade to the Caesar cipher, turning the simple shift of a single alphabet into shifts across multiple alphabets to increase complexity. For more on the Caesar cipher, see the Caesar Cipher Edition.  
The main difference between the Vigenère and Caesar ciphers is that in the Vigenère cipher, the relationship between the ciphertext and plaintext letters isn't one-to-one, making the key harder to figure out. However, since it still follows patterns, you can still find repeating ciphertext segments. By using high-frequency words in common language, you can guess the plaintext for specific segments of ciphertext, deduce the key length from the spacing between repeats, and analyze the key.  
However, if you don't have much text, this can be difficult. In these situations, you should use other methods to guess the key. The personality, past, and writing habits of the person who made the code are important clues. Here is a list of people at Laplace who are very likely to play pranks with ciphers:

Adler, Ulrich, X, Richard, Dawkins, Victor ...

If you encounter a prank, consider these suspects first. If you can't crack the code, confront the suspects directly and ask them to stop the prank. If a suspect denies involvement, try to force them to decode it for you. It doesn't matter whether they're really the culprit or not.

### 3610106 · Pending Report

**Preliminary Investigation Report on the Toybox**

After the ninth "Storm," Musae III provided the toybox as a tool for simulation experiments to help develop a Pre-Storm Emergency Protocol that meets LSCC standards while adhering to humanitarian principles.  
Note: Existing solutions all failed to meet LSCC standards. Based on the scientific principles of "objective investigation, rational skepticism, pluralistic doubt, and equal debate," response plans for the "Storm" should also be tested in practice. Thus, this is not a useless effort or an excuse to play board games at work.  
This project, led by Researcher Ulrich and supervised by Researcher Adler, launched after the ninth "Storm" and has been gradually refined. During this period, a technical fault in the box caused a large-scale "language disorder" phenomenon, which stabilized after Researcher Adler's intervention, preventing major losses.  
During the "Free Breeze" incident, Researcher Ulrich used preliminary toybox research results—"Artificial Storm" technology—to test the "Regulus" record, achieving significant results.  
Afterward, due to busy schedules, development of the Pre-Storm Emergency Protocol was temporarily put on hold.  
After the tenth "Storm," new anomalies began appearing within the toybox. Researchers Ulrich and Adler disagreed on how to handle the toybox, each submitting separate research reports. These reports are currently being compared and evaluated; specific details are expected in future publications.

### 3810101 · Speech Duel

**Q&A with the Checkered Gray-and-White**

First Meeting Minutes on the Point Irreversible

Committee Member A: I maintain that exploring the Point Irreversible now is reckless. It would waste time and resources we don't have to spare. These are extraordinary times, Ms. Kenna.  
Kenna: Precisely because these are extraordinary times, Committee Member. We can't afford the luxury of overlooking a known exception.  
Committee Member A: I think we have different definitions of waste, Ms. Kenna. I cannot agree to a risk like this—  
Kenna: I prefer to call it necessary sowing, Committee Member. Plant a seed, and soon enough, it bears fruit.  
Committee Member B: You've spent too long in Glasgow, Ms. Kenna. Does everyone there speak in metaphors?  
Kenna: Would you like to discuss rhetoric with me, Committee Member? I could start with Georgian literature—though I doubt either of us wants that.  
Kenna: I believe surveying the Point Irreversible is necessary, and it will shape what comes next.  
Kenna: One Point Irreversible means there could be more. As you said, our focus is fighting against Manus Vindictae. That's exactly why we need these strongholds, Committee Member.  
Kenna: We can't be an island in times like these.  
Kenna: Sending a boat out toward the Point Irreversible is the first step to making sure we're not.

### 3810104 · Read Something Fresh

**Sequel · UTTU Scouting Trip**

Crossing most of that vast building—the long corridors, the broad staircases, the open ground beneath its fallen dome—left me nearly spent. You can probably guess how I felt. I checked my notebook of hurried sketches one last time and told myself: "Just a little further—that tavern drink the locals talked about is waiting for you."  
Unsurprisingly, the "neighbors" were at the tavern too. The old woman behind the bar mixed me a quick drink. Beside me sat a man in an old postal uniform, blank-faced, his shoulders worn pale. A ragged, half-transparent canvas mailbag rested on his lap. It seemed I had a "ghost" for company.  
"You're welcome to move seats if he makes you uneasy. Old Michał never listens to anyone anyway," the old woman offered gently.  
I thanked her but stayed put. It was, after all, a good chance to rest while observing a "ghost." The old postman's shade sat in silence, staring at the grain of the bar counter, as if an after-work vodka should have been waiting there. Not far off, an old woman wrapped in a heavy shawl stood by a table, taking out the same photo—a photo-shaped shade—again and again, turning it over in her hands. I guessed she must have lived alone in some apartment block, in a room that always smelled of coal stove and old cloth.  
By the time I finished my drink and looked back, a man in work clothes was standing at the center of the empty tavern—I couldn't say when he'd arrived—as if he'd been waiting there for a hundred years, a thousand years. Half his face was burned, and I caught the faint scent of ash coming off him.  
Something about his face must have drawn me in, because I found myself standing, trying to speak with him.  
"Sir, if I may ask, what are you doing here?"  
"Waiting." His answer came out hoarse.  
"Waiting for whom?"  
"A woman with eyes as blue as Lake Baikal."  
"You're hurt, sir."  
"No ... this isn't a wound."  
"It's Her ... it's proof that God has forsaken us."  
He raised a hand to the burn scarring his cheek.  
"We were promised gold. That year, winter went on forever. I waved her goodbye and headed into the frozen wild.  
The workers labored on, day and night.  
Until the black oil erupted from the wells, flames rising and fading in the falling snow."  
His pupils trembled faintly, a silent fire burning somewhere in him.  
"Our sky broke open—but no dawn ever came."  
I think I knew where he'd come from. Everyone in this country knew where he and his scars came from.

### 3810105 · What Was Lost and Retrieved

**The Stone Remembers a New Voice**

"Ma'am, I have something for you. I found it in the residential wing."  
A faint clink of metal chain. The rustle of cloth.  
"There it is. I thought I'd lost it for good."  
"I opened it by accident and saw the photo inside. You were beautiful back then. Eyes like lake water."  
"Thank you, Stasia."  
The click of metal opening. A gasp.  
"While we were surveying the building, we came across a man with burn scars on his face ... He told us he was waiting for a woman with eyes as blue as Lake Baikal."  
"Ah. So you've already met Tomasz."  
Another rustle of cloth. Knuckles clenching. A soft sigh.  
"He's always wandering this building—the stairs, the landings, my tavern. He's still wearing that old wool coat he had on the day I saw him off at the station.  
Even though he's long dead. What wanders here is just his ghost, his echo, whatever you want to call it—repeating the same few lines over and over even when I'm standing right in front of him."  
"I'm so ..."  
"No need to apologize, Stasia. You found my pendant for me—if anything, I owe you a few more drinks.  
"Don't worry ... that's just how life goes. Tomasz proposed to me once. He wanted to go work the far-off oil fields and save up enough to come back and marry me. He never made it back. When they brought me the scraps of his clothes, I thought that was the end of everything. When I was exiled, I thought that was the end too ... And when the anomaly hit, I really thought it was over this time. But here I am. Even Tomasz is still here.  
"So ... life still goes on."

### 3810107 · Childhood Fragment

**A Page in the Diary**

Sat Sunny  
We got a rare good day today! The sky's still gray, but I could actually see the sun. I woke up early, since Róża and I had agreed to meet at the flowerbed. I'd packed sliced black bread and pickles into my bag the night before. I couldn't wait, and after breakfast I ran straight there. Róża was already waiting for me, but Emil and Andrzej were late. We waited ten minutes for them, and Róża talked with me the whole time.  
We took the tram to the newly built steel factory. Emil and Andrzej wanted to see the chimney, and I wanted to sketch. It really was a huge chimney, like some beast breathing coal-fire smoke from its great mouth. Róża said that after all these years, Nowa Miedź finally having a factory this size is a good thing. I drew the chimney, the canal, and a few wildflowers. My river sketchbook keeps getting thicker. Andrzej said he thinks I'll become a river expert. But it's not the canal itself I want to draw, though it matters too, of course. Why do we build our houses by the river? Why do we build our factories by the river? Why does the canal bring more people here? Why does the river turn black? Could we live somewhere better? Róża's explained it all to me before, but I want to find the answers myself. Maybe once I've figured it all out, I can help Mum, Dad, and my friends live better lives.

### 3810108 · Long Journey

**The Story of a Brick**

When I was still a student, I spent a year traveling, visiting buildings. I wandered the abandoned amphitheaters of Rome, its aqueducts and temples, sketching in the Mediterranean wind the way Brunelleschi and Donatello did in the 15th century. I was trying to find the essence, the true form of architecture, in those collapsed, unadorned ruins.  
Until at last I arrived in India. At the Indian Institute of Management, I met an old man with scars on his face. He told me a story about a brick and an arch.  
The architect asks the brick, "Brick, what do you want to be?"  
The brick says, "I want to be an arch."  
The architect says, "I want an arch too, but arches are too expensive. What if we use a concrete lintel instead?"  
Still, the brick tells you only this: "I want to be an arch."  
At the end of the story, the old man said, "It's important to respect the material you've chosen. If you're going to use this brick, the best way is to respect it, to let it become what it is, not just to make do with it."

### 3810109 · New Life

**A Hidden Letter**

Dear Olga,  
My warmest greetings to you, old friend!  
You might be surprised to get this letter. Marek and I wrote it together because we thought you'd want to know how we're doing. And we wanted to talk to you too—to our dear old neighbor, someone who still remembers that we come from Nowa Miedź.  
We're doing well in Gdańsk. The sky isn't gray here. We've rented a house near the north port and started a small business. Please, don't worry about us.  
It's hard to put into words how I survived those first years. At least three times a week, I'd wake up crying because I'd dreamed of that day, the last time I saw Lucjan. Even now, I still feel sick when I see one of those cattle cars. It was cars just like that one that swallowed my child. They split families up between different groups, and by the time I finally got off that train alive, I'd lost my son forever.  
Forgive me for writing about such painful things. But Lucjan loved you so much. I think about it often—if we could go back to Nowa Miedź, how he'd run straight into your arms, how he'd introduce you to his little sister. He never got to meet her. Maybe he's watching her from somewhere above.  
Which reminds me, I should introduce you to the newest member of our family—Maja. She was born two winters ago. I've included a photo of her with this letter. I wanted you to see her for yourself. She has my nose, Marek's eyes, and ears just like Lucjan's, the same shape that came down from my grandmother. I think Lucjan would be happy for us. He always wanted a little sister.  
We asked around for a long time, and finally a fellow countryman told us your address hadn't changed at all—still the one I remembered. I can't really describe how that felt to hear. You're a brave woman, Olga, and that's such a rare thing these days. We've all weathered the storm. No one escapes its ravages. I wish you health and good fortune, and that you and your family are well. Marek and I think of you often, and we hope that someday we'll see each other again.  
Always thinking of you,  
Katarzyna

### 3810110 · The Wake

**A Late Funeral**

They left a small gap around the doors and windows. This building has plenty of windows. If it could hold voices in, then departing souls should be able to pass through those same gaps and reach heaven, or so the elders said. The young architect nodded and covered the mirror, as her hometown's traditions demanded—so the living wouldn't glimpse their reflection and trap the dead among them.  
Someone should have done this for that boy years ago. He was left behind in the train car, among the exiled, in the very building he'd once lived in. This was a funeral long overdue.  
Jasio put on his best suit, the only foreign wool one he owned. Everyone who'd known the boy dressed in their finest. There was no coffin at this funeral, but people still gathered at the little bar and kept vigil for the boy all through the night. Candlelight rose soft and warm, lighting up the seat where he used to sit.  
"You're singing," said the girl with the hand puppet. "What should I sing?"  
They taught her how to sing a dirge. A wake needed no instruments, only song after song.  
At dawn, the people set out from the bar with white candles in hand, heading for the dome. The boy had wanted to see that dome finished.  
"Say something, Stasia. He'd want to hear it from you," the old woman said softly.  
The architect stepped forward and cleared her throat. The words caught there like fine sand—an itch she couldn't clear.  
"I won't forget Lucjan." She began with those words, as sunlight spilled gently through the broken dome, and it made her think of the light that boy had become. "He ... he's with us."

### 3810112 · Something New

**Clappy Jelly User Manual**

Name:  
Clappy Jelly

Features:  
① Monitors the local environment's energy levels in real time, precisely detecting spatial fluctuations.  
② Automatically triggers a continuous alarm when abnormal or dangerous energy signals appear.

How to Use:  
① Place Clappy Jelly steadily at the center of the target monitoring area.  
② Massage its tentacles until the sensor pads glow green.  
③ Pat the top of its head for about 3 seconds. It will power on and float up slowly to its working height.  
④ Clap hard once, and it will expand a spherical monitoring field, gradually widening its range.  
⑤ Clap hard once more, and it will lock in the current sphere size and begin monitoring energy.  
⑥ While the environment's energy stays stable, Clappy Jelly will cruise slowly on its own. If it detects abnormal fluctuations, its tentacles will clap rapidly, much as a nervous person might, sounding the alarm with a frightened cry.  
⑦ Sing Clappy Jelly a lullaby, and it will descend slowly, save its monitoring data, and drift off to sleep.

Manufacturer:  
Institutum Lorentz

Notice:  
Please do not forcibly power down this device during large-scale arcane rituals.  
Clappy Jelly is professional, but not especially brave. If startled, it may enter a prolonged alert state.

### 3810113 · Before the Rain

**"Living Electricity System"**

"If blood turns to electric current, does the soul still stay in the body?"

The blood vessels change first. They stop their soft pulsing and begin to grow straight, silent, climbing upward along the wrists—red, yellow, blue, green, black, and white gelcoat sheathing thin threads of metal, pushing slowly through skin, through flesh.

The bakery clerk is the first to notice something wrong. She stops touching hot water. When the morning steam rises, her arms give off a faint glow, and in the places where blood should flow, something else now coils and branches quietly, like a tangle of wiring rewoven into place.

More and more people fill the hospitals. X-rays line the light boxes. Inside the pale bodies there are no organs, only crisscrossing wires, like the vast, silent power grid beneath a city. The doctors say the humming in the chest, the numbness in the neck, and the teeth that flicker with light now and then—all of it is nothing but insomnia.

Then even the chapel starts fizzing and crackling with prayer. The silver ornament on the host's chest is leaking current, throwing off tiny blue sparks. Someone weeps on their knees. Someone catches the smell of scorched metal in the air. An old woman looks up and quietly asks the question that has long lingered within her heart:

"If blood turns to electric current, does the soul still stay in the body?"

The host doesn't answer. A patch of bronze has surfaced on his palm too.

### 3810114 · Official Document

**Record on the Requisition of the Nowa Miedź Point Irreversible Building and the Establishment of the Eastern Europe Branch**

Clearance Level: Top Secret

Record on the Requisition of the Nowa Miedź Point Irreversible Building and the Establishment of the Eastern Europe Branch  
Attendee List: Lucy, Irinei, Kenna, Ida, Finn ... (and other authorized associated personnel). All personnel listed above must strictly comply with the confidentiality agreement.

In accordance with the Temporal Anomaly Regional Intervention Regulations and related requisition management procedures, the following is recorded regarding the elevation of the former "St. Pavlov Foundation Nowa Miedź Office" to the status of Eastern Europe Branch:

I. Background  
The Temporal Anomaly is a highly dangerous arcane spatiotemporal hazard. Any individual exposed within its area of effect will vanish instantly, without a trace.  
A building in the Nowa Miedź region holds extremely high strategic value. It has been confirmed that this building is capable of stably resisting the regressive effects of the Temporal Anomaly. Individuals within its enclosed structure are immune to the Temporal Anomaly's effects.

II. Establishment of the Office  
On November 8, 1996, the St. Pavlov Foundation Nowa Miedź Office was formally completed. Its primary responsibilities include monitoring abnormal environmental energy, coordinating resident evacuations, and handling regional anomalous incidents.  
Across multiple disaster events, this facility has operated stably, with no reversion breach incidents recorded.

III. Formal Requisition  
On January 18, 1986, the Foundation formally initiated the permanent requisition process, bringing the building under core facility management. Structural and functional renovations followed, including a rebuilt disaster-prevention system, the addition of research and containment areas, and safety upgrades to public spaces.  
All former residents were properly resettled, and the corresponding community functions were transferred accordingly.

IV. Establishment of the Branch  
On ██ ██, 19██, all renovation work was completed. With a portion of the public space preserved, the site was formally established as:  
"St. Pavlov Foundation Eastern Europe Branch"  
The former designation, "Nowa Miedź Office," is discontinued as of this date. All related files and authorizations are consolidated into the Eastern Europe Branch system.

Recorded accordingly.

St. Pavlov Foundation Central Council  
██ ██, 19██

### 3810115 · Growth of Order

**Excerpt from the Storm Act (Temporary)**

Excerpt from the Storm Act (Temporary)  
Before the term "Storm" was formally established, the Foundation used "Temporal Anomaly" as the umbrella term for this class of phenomena. Anomalous incidents, including time reversal, the disappearance of buildings, the erasure of populations, and historical discontinuities, were filed separately for a long time. It was only after the Nowa Miedź Project that the Foundation confirmed these were manifestations of the same systemic disaster and began drafting the first edition of the "Storm" Regional Intervention Regulations.

I. Confirmation of Terminology  
The "Storm" is provisionally confirmed as a highly dangerous disaster whose core manifestations are time reversal, historical erosion, and the reconstruction of reality.  
"Temporal Anomaly" is retained solely as a legacy term from earlier records.  
...  
II. Duties of the Foundation  
The Supplementary Act on the Foundation's Duties specifies the following:  
The Foundation bears responsibility for the research and early warning of the "Storm," the takeover of endangered zones, personnel evacuation, the preservation of historical civilizations, and the humanitarian restoration of survivors' identities.  
The primary bearer of responsibility and the details of implementation remain under dispute. The portions that have been finalized are recorded as follows:  
...  
At this stage, "Storm" research prioritizes early-warning mechanisms, with continued work toward advancing and refining the "Storm" emergency response system, and ongoing exploration into the possibility of crossing or ending the "Storm" ...  
...  
A system of disclosure rights concerning "Storm"-related information should be established, with strict controls against leaks of classified information, and close attention paid to staff panic, despair, and other emotional strain ...  
...

### 30510102 · A Reliable Friend

**An Unfinished Letter**

There hasn't been much progress these last few days, but I studied the cipher again and decided that light was the answer—it was the only thing that could cause a reaction on the frescos. I'm going to re-design the experiment, but I need some inspiration. Could you collect more information about the frescos for me? Their locations, contents, number—anything will do.  
And I have some bad news: you are not the only one who wants me to crack the puzzle. Right before I wrote this letter, I managed to escape from a group of Templars. I'll set off for Florence in days, and ... (The letter abruptly stops here, as if someone interrupted the writer)

### 30510103 · A Rare Apple

**The "Golden Apple"**

Ezio,  
We are still looking for information about the "Golden Apple," as what we have cannot confirm its connection with the Piece of Eden. Word is that it has something to do with the secret chamber that used to house the Compliners. Whatever it is, it has drawn a lot of attention and you should be prepared for trouble.  
And there's one more thing you should know: we've heard the apple snore like a human several times, which is unbelievable. Some said the Piece of Eden came alive over the past few years, but I highly doubt it.  
Finally, we couldn't find Leonardo in Milan and I think he's in trouble. I'll keep you posted.

Niccolò Machiavelli

### 30510105 · A Colorful Story

**Diary with Paint on It**

March ■■  
Good heavens! I never thought linear perspective would be so hard to learn. I asked plenty of dumb questions, but Uccello answered all of them patiently and kept encouraging me.  
Father said I needed more practice too.

March ■■  
The lords kept throwing out new ideas, but Uccello always managed to talk them down. The fresco is getting more and more lively and artistic ... It is an honor to witness the birth of a masterpiece.

March ■■  
Following Uccello's advice, everyone tried that magical paint, including the most stubborn ones among us.  
I hope these days never end.

April ■■  
We are being dismissed.  
My skills have improved over these last few days. Uccello generously shared his paint with us before he left.  
The story is coming to an end. We have to leave the painting unfinished ...  
I've put away the paint, but its odor remains in my mind.

### 30510107 · The Stalker

**The Book Merchant's Notebook**

[Southeast Corner of the Room]  
Piles of a sand-quicklime mixture, a bit of carbon dust, several nails and punched paintings.  
Just some leftover materials they didn't use. Worthless to me.

[East of the Room]  
Two carving knives, several brushes, and a piece of glass.  
Nothing worth taking.  
The glass looks like some kind of lens.

Dried Paint  
They didn't seal the paint can with beeswax! What a waste ...

A dozen letters and several intact paintings.  
Finally, something worth taking.  
I can make a new manuscript and name it Mystery of the Frescos or Wonders of Florence ... No, I need a more eye-catching title.

[North of the Room]  
Scaffoldings.  
Collapsed.

Messy carvings: a group of dancing human symbols, their meaning is unclear.  
Is this a signature? I bet the scaffoldings were broken by the ones who were fighting.

### 30510108 · Corner of the Secret Chamber

**An Unsent Letter**

Dear Uccello,  
It's been too long since we parted. I heard you went back to Pratovecchio. Is it still as beautiful as you said it was? I still remember what it looked like in your painting—a sea of olive and fig trees.  
Those days we spent together were just like a dream. I've always wanted to pick up the brush once again, but my life is already occupied by meaningless housework ... They said I didn't have much time left. (Blurred)

But I came back without letting anyone know. When I left a new signature at the corner, I found that our friends had done the same thing. Lippi, Giovanni ... What about you? I'm never sure since you've always been so unpredictable.

How are you doing? Hopefully, you're still drawing.  
In fact, I know you are.

Bianca

### 30510109 · Picnic Blanket

**Observation Experiment Record**

"According to the Observer Instruction Book, we've met the conditions for terminating the observation."  
"But the picnic is not over yet ... Do we have to go?"  
"I'm afraid it's not an option. Now, Ms. Sotheby, Mr. APPLe, I need you to focus. You'll be leaving the stage at any time ... Hello, Mr. APPLe?"  
"He's not responding. Oh no, is he poisoned again? Should I make an antidote?!"  
"No need. I think he's just drunk."  
"But X said if an observer ended an observation when unconscious, they might be directly transferred to another observation. I think we'd better wake him up, Ms. Sotheby."  
"Mr. APPLe? Wake up! We need to ..."  
"No ..."

## 章节 385101

### 38510101 · X's Experiment Record

**X's Experiment Record**

Quantum Superpositions, Simulated Worlds, Space-time BubblesWe need more data of the Stage for further analysis.  
The Huygens-Fresnel Interferometer produces sufficient chaos particles to generate virtual yet observable spaces through consciousness fluctuations. Although the mechanism remains unclear, we have no intention of investigating into it, considering the Pandora's Boxes we have opened before. Therefore, we have decided to focus on the data of the users' brains—the performers who will be going onto the Stage. Fortunately, we have a friend who excels at monitoring brain activity; with her help, we are almost certain to obtain valuable data.  
We are aware that introducing a new variable to the system could result in even more chaos and complexities, but we predict that the outcome will be beneficial for our research.

### 38510102 · News Broadcast

**News Broadcast**

... a major agricultural achievement, these wheat seeds developed in the Vavilov Complex have received widespread adoption. At this stage, increasing crop yield is but a rudimentary goal. Our nation's scientists are now working on improving crop adaptiveness to eliminate hunger on Earth and bring us one step closer to terraforming Mars. Note that a large number of Bumblebee drones will be delivering new farm machinery and organic polymer to the croplands today. Please mind your safety when using aerial transportation. That's all for today's morning news. Thank you for listening. Have a nice day!

### 38510103 · International Polymer Summit

**International Polymer Summit**

Dear friends, researchers, and comrades who care for the well-being of humanity, welcome to the International Polymer Summit!  
We sincerely invite you to visit our complexes, the Soviet Union's most advanced scientific facilities. You will discover the most cutting-edge technologies of our era and witness how they are integrated into everyday production and life to truly benefit every learner and worker. From genetic engineering in agriculture to robotic automation in industry, from the transmission of knowledge and experience to breakthroughs in thought and computation, the great foundation behind all of these achievements is the central focus of this summit—polymer.  
We are delighted to share and promote these technologies for the benefit of humanity across the world. Visitors are kindly requested to collect a vehicle activation key from the nearest Rafik and travel by Turbine to the summit venue. We look forward to your arrival.

### 38510104 · Objection

**Objection**

As the representative of the Politburo, I have serious concerns about the safety of Comrade Sechenov's proposal. Not all innovations are beneficial to our country and institution. We have learned from history that technologies—especially the most advanced technologies—can cause enormous disasters if left unsupervised. Do we fully understand the technologies involved in this proposal? Is there a regulatory body to provide proper oversight? Is Comrade Sechenov able to take responsibility for the possible consequences? Until these questions can be answered, this proposal should remain in the laboratory. I believe what we currently have is enough to serve our country and society.

—Yegor Timofeyevich Molotov  
Date: ■■■■/■■/■■

### 38510105 · Lebedev's Notes

**Lebedev's Notes**

The auto weapon factory, aka the repair vendor, is complete. To increase its functions, I have added a variety of algorithms to its thinking module, making it a genuine artificial intelligence. I am sure the entire Mendeleev Complex will be proud of this achievement because it—or should I say she?—has developed her own emotions! We have not figured out whether this is simply imitation, but all the scientists in the lab are excited about it. Perhaps we should give her a feminine name, a lovely one with an innocent feel ... Yes, NORA. That's a good name.
